(559)    Fifth, a potential foreclosure strategy would have no significant detrimental effect on
         competition because bundling is a common practice in the industry. Therefore, customers
         will be able to rely on alternative packaged deals from other international and local
         suppliers and could develop counterstrategies such as creating or commissioning content
         for their own use (as a number of market players have done over the last few years). (590)

6.6.5.    Conglomerate relationship between the wholesale supply of FTA TV sport channels and
          the neighbouring segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s channels in
          Germany

6.6.5.1. The Notifying Party’s views
(571)     The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in a foreclosure
          strategy for the following reasons.
(572)     First, the Parties do not consider it appropriate to segment the wholesale supply of TV
          channels between FTA and pay TV, especially in relation to sports in Germany. Warner
          offers an FTA TV Eurosport channel that shows lower-cost sports rights, including non-
          exclusive content (e.g., ‘Born for this’ Women’s German Football Team Documentary)
          and highlights. However, other players active in pay TV and streaming offer the majority
          of overall sports audiences: Sky holds the rights of Bundesliga (the German domestic
          football league), Formula 1 and golf (including all the Majors), and DAZN holds the
          rights of the Bundesliga (games not shown by Sky) and the Champions League in
          Germany. Therefore, looking solely at FTA sports channels in Germany omits these
          constraints. (605)
(573)     Second, the Parties lack market power in the supply of FTA TV sports channels in
          Germany, given the strong constraint from the market leader Sport1 Meiden ([50-60]%)

                                                           115
         and a range of SVOD operators and on-demand platforms such as DAZN, Amazon Prime
         Video, and YouTube. (606)
(574)    Third, the Parties also lack any incentive to engage in a foreclosure strategy, since
         MVPDs will react by dropping the Parties’ TV channels and by shifting to alternative
         content options from linear or non-linear competitors. Additionally, as the Parties do not
         offer pay TV channels at a retail level, they would not have a platform to which they
         could drive the dropped linear TV channels. (607)
(575)    Fourth, a potential foreclosure strategy would have no significant detrimental effect on
         competition because bundling is a common practice in the industry. Therefore, customers
         will be able to rely on alternative packaged deals from other international and local
         suppliers and could develop counterstrategies such as creating or commissioning content
         for their own use (as a number of market players have done over the last few years). (608)

6.6.5.2. Commission’s assessment
(576)    First, the Commission considers that the merged entity will not have the ability to
         foreclose competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels by leveraging its market power
         in the segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV sports channels to strengthen its position
         in the neighbouring segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s channels in
         Germany. According to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, for foreclosure to be a
         concern, the merged entity must have a significant degree of market power in one of the
         markets concerned. (609) The Commission considers that the merged entity does not have
         a significant degree of market power in the market for the wholesale supply of FTA TV
         sports channels in Germany.
(577)    In the first place, the Commission notes that the Transaction gives rise to a combined
         market share of [40-50]% in the segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV sports
         channels in Germany. Warner has a [40-50]% market share and Paramount is not active
         in this segment. (610)
(578)    In the second place, the Parties are constrained by the presence of a strong competitor,
         Sport 1 Meiden, which has a market share of [50-60]%. (611)
(579)    In the third place, there is competitive pressure from SVOD services. (612) Moreover,
         according to the majority of respondents to the market investigation, retail TV
         distributors and MVPDs negotiate simultaneously for access to linear TV channels and
         non-linear SVOD services. (613) This indicates that a strong position in SVOD services
         can create significant competitive pressure during those negotiations. Market
         respondents (i.e., competitors and vertically integrated entertainment companies) active
         in Germany explained the importance of acquiring both non-linear and linear services:
         ‘we always try and most of the time succeed in acquiring linear and non-linear rights
         since our subscribers want to view the content in a linear and non-linear format’ and
         ‘distributors typically seek a range of ways of delivering content to their customers,
         which includes linear and on-demand services (whether catch up or a separate SVOD
         environment)’. (614)

                                                         116
(580)    Additionally, several market participants indicated that SVOD is ‘a true alternative to
         TV channels’ and that they ‘consider SVOD services an alternative to tv-channels in
         general. So do the distributors, which is why there is a very systematic and structural
         change happening where the offering from distributors/MVPD is more and more based
         on SVOD services and not only on tv-channels’. (615)
(581)    In the fourth place, MVPDs seeking to acquire TV channels are able to switch between
         different wholesale suppliers of TV channels. MVPDs may replace channels with
         alternative TV channels from the same genre or different genres. (616) In fact, the majority
         of respondents of the market investigation indicated that the acquisition decision of TV
         channels is based on a multi-genre approach, rather than on the genre of a particular TV
         channel. (617) In particular, a market participant active in the German market indicated
         that ‘[s]topping a type of channels would not necessarily entail [the respondent] to
         replace them with channels of the exact same genre. The main objective is to build
         attractive, rich and diverse offers that reply to prospect customers and our subscribers
         needs and expectations’. (618)
(582)    In the fifth place, the Commission notes the argument of the Parties that MVPDs hold
         significant bargaining power, as they have the ability to drop a channel and replace it
         with alternatives, substantial customer basis and developed packaging capabilities. (619)
         Under this assumption, the merged entity would not have the ability to force bundles on
         MVPDs, as enforcing bundles could lead to a risk of switching to other alternatives or
         remove the channels altogether. (620)
(583)    Second, most market participants active in Germany that have expressed concerns in the
         market investigation indicate that the Transaction may cause ‘bundling pressure’ and that
         ‘content providers typically do not permit the unbundling / separation of channel
         offerings, such that distributors are required to carry the full suite of channels, even
         where these are not valued equally’. Nonetheless, responses are nuanced, as one of the
         participants explained that ‘this dynamic is evolving in light of the growth of streaming
         services. For example, [the respondent] has observed in some instances that where it has
         committed to carrying a content provider’s SVOD service, the provider may adopt a more
         flexible approach in relation to the associated linear channel offerings’. (621) The
         Commission considers that such concerns are linked to an increase in market power.
         However, as indicated above, the Commission considers that the merged entity does not
         have a significant degree of market power in the market for the wholesale supply of FTA
         TV sports channels in Germany.
(584)    Third, given that the conditions under the Non-Horizontal Guidelines are
         cumulative, (622) the Commission does not need to take a position on whether the two
         other conditions are satisfied.

6.6.5.3. Conclusion
(585)     For all the above reasons, the Commission concludes that the Transaction will not raise
          serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a result of a conglomerate
          foreclosure strategy in the market for the wholesale supply of FTA TV sport channels
          and the neighbouring segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s channels in
          Germany, considering the merged entity would not have the ability to engage in such a
          strategy.

6.6.6.    Conglomerate relationship between the wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s channels
          and the neighbouring segment for wholesale supply of pay TV children’s channels in
          Spain

6.6.6.1. The Notifying Party’s views
(586)     The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in a foreclosure
          strategy for the following reasons.
(587)     First, the Parties lack market power in the supply of FTA TV channels in Spain, given
          modest shares and strong competitors. The Parties combined market share exceeds the
          30% threshold marginally in the segment for FTA TV children’s channels in Spain. Post-
          Transaction, the Parties will continue to be constrained by players such as TVE
          ([30-40]%), Disney ([20-30]%), ETB ([0-5]%) and Televisio de Catalunya ([0-5]%) and
          by a range of SVOD operators (e.g. Netflix, Amazon Prime Video, Disney+, Apple TV,
          Crunchyroll, Atresplayer Premium, Rakuten TV, Shahid and Tivify). (623)
(588)     Second, MVPDs’ bargaining power will remain undiminished post-Transaction, as they
          are large TV providers with substantial customer bases. When they drop a channel,
          replacements may include traditional FTA TV channels, FAST channels or multi-genre
          SVOD services. (624) Additionally, negotiations with MVPDs are carried out on a
          portfolio basis, often including SVOD services, and the Parties compete to offer the most
          attractive bundle of channels and services. Additionally, the Parties do not have material
          carriage agreements for children’s pay TV channels only, and they do not have separate
          personnel or teams who only distribute channels for children. Therefore, an analysis
          limited to children’s channels does not reflect commercial reality. (625)
(589)     Third, Warner’s share in FTA TV channels derives from its 49% interest in the Boing
          S.p.A joint-venture (majority-owned by Mediaset), which reduces the ability for the
          merged entity to make unilateral decisions. (626)
(590)     Fourth, any attempt post-Transaction to worsen competitive conditions with respect to
          linear TV channels would only serve to accelerate the global trend towards non-linear
          services. Therefore, wholesale suppliers of TV channels do not possess any market power
          to profitably raise prices or worsen terms. (627)
(591)     Fifth, the merged entity will lack the incentive to engage in anticompetitive bundling or
          leveraging between FTA TV and pay TV channels in Spain. There are generally no
          revenues attached to FTA TV channels, as they are monetized through advertising
          revenues. The Parties would therefore only stand to lose from not offering their FTA TV

         channels to retailers or MVPDs if they are unwilling to carry the channels in a
         bundle. (628)

6.6.6.2. Commission’s assessment
(592)    First, the Commission considers that the merged entity will not have the ability to
         foreclose competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels by leveraging its market power
         in the segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s channels to strengthen its
         position in the neighbouring segment for wholesale supply of pay TV children’s channels
         in Spain. According to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, for foreclosure to be a
         concern, the merged entity must have a significant degree of market power in one of the
         markets concerned. (629) The Commission considers that the merged entity does not have
         a significant degree of market power in the market for the wholesale supply of FTA TV
         children’s channels in Spain.
(593)    In the first place, the Commission notes that the Transaction gives rise to a combined
         market share of [30-40]% market share in the segment for wholesale supply of FTA TV
         children’s channels in Spain, which only exceeds 30% by a small margin. Warner has a
         [30-40]% market share and Paramount is not active in this segment. (630)
(594)    In the second place, the Parties are constrained by the presence of various competitors.
         In particular, TVE has a market share of [30-40]%, Disney has a market share of
         [20-30]%, ETB has a market share of [0-5]% and Televisio de Catalunya with a market
         share of [0-5]%. (631)
(595)    In the third place, there is competitive pressure from SVOD services. (632) In fact, most
         respondents to the market investigation considered that SVOD services offering
         children’s      content      are   alternatives      to    dedicated     children’s    TV
                     633
         channels. ( ) Additionally, market participants active in Spain indicated that: ‘[w]hile
         a linear AV offer is easy to use by children, SVoD services which offer content directed
         (mainly) at children can be seen as alternatives’ and that ‘[v]iewing habits and
         consumption have changed; SVOD content is particularly attractive for children as they
         tend to consume large volumes of shorter content in one sitting, the content is easier to
         navigate on the user interface than finding it in linear channels on the EPG, and the lack
         of advertising holds their attention more’. (634)
(596)    In the fourth place, MVPDs seeking to acquire TV channels are able to switch between
         different wholesale suppliers of TV channels or SVOD providers. MVPDs may replace
         channels with alternative TV channels from the same genre or different genres. (635) In
         fact, the majority of respondents of the market investigation indicated that the acquisition
         decision of TV channels is based on a multi-genre approach, rather than on the genre of
         a particular TV channel. (636) A market participant active in the Spanish market indicated
         that replacing a channel could be modulated by several factors such as ‘broader shifts in

         our business model (such as reallocating that investment towards SVOD integration
         rather than a linear channel)’. (637)
(597)    In the fifth place, the Commission notes the argument of the Parties that MVPDs hold
         significant bargaining power, as they have the ability to drop a channel and replace it
         with alternatives, substantial customer basis and developed packaging capabilities. (638)
         Under this assumption, the merged entity would not have the ability to force bundles on
         MVPDs, as enforcing bundles could lead to a risk of switching to other alternatives or
         remove the channels altogether. (639) For instance, in August 2025, Vodafone Spain
         dropped Paramount’s channels, replacing them with a mix of FTA, pay TV and FAST
         channels. Moreover, in January 2025, Movistar+ Spain replaced AMC Networks’
         channels with a package of BBC FAST channels. (640)
(598)    Second, most market participants active in Spain that have expressed concerns in the
         market investigation indicate that the Transaction may cause ‘bundling pressure’ and that
         ‘[f]orcing MVPDs to acquire their entire portfolio not only secures minimum revenue
         guarantees, but it also guarantees maximum distribution reach for all their properties,
         which is essential to sustain their advertising business’. (641) The Commission considers
         that such concerns are linked to an increase in market power. However, as indicated
         above, the Commission considers that the merged entity does not have a significant
         degree of market power in the market for the wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s
         channels in Spain.
(599)    Third, given that the conditions under the Non-Horizontal Guidelines are
         cumulative, (642) the Commission does not need to take a position on whether the two
         other conditions are satisfied.

6.6.6.3. Conclusion
(600)    For all the above reasons, the Commission concludes that the Transaction will not raise
         serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a result of a conglomerate
         foreclosure strategy in the market for the wholesale supply of FTA TV children’s
         channels and the neighbouring segment for wholesale supply of pay TV children’s
         channels in Spain, considering the merged entity would not have the ability to engage in
         such a strategy.


7.       COMMITMENTS

7.1.     Analytical framework
(601)    The following principles from the Merger Regulation and the Commission’s Notice on
         Remedies acceptable under Council Regulation (EC) No 139/2004 and under
         Commission Regulation (EC) No 802/2004 (the ‘Remedies Notice’) (643) apply where
         parties to a concentration offer commitments with a view to rendering a concentration
         compatible with the internal market.

        https://www.elespanol.com/corazon/bluper/20241221/giro-grados-movistar-plus-sustitutos-tomaran-
        relevo-canales-amc/910409069 0.html (last accessed on 12 July 2026).

(643)   OJ C 267, 22.10.2008, p.1.
                                                         120
(602)    Where a notified concentration raises serious doubts as to its compatibility with the
         internal market, the parties to the concentration may undertake to modify the
         concentration to remove the grounds for the serious doubts identified by the Commission.
         Pursuant to Article 6(2) of the Merger Regulation, where the Commission finds that,
         following modification by the undertakings concerned, a notified concentration no longer
         raises serious doubts, it shall declare the concentration compatible with the internal
         market pursuant to Article 6(1)(b) of the Merger Regulation in conjunction with
         Article 6(2) of the Merger Regulation.
(603)    As set out in the Remedies Notice, the commitments proposed by the parties have to
         eliminate the competition concerns entirely and have to be comprehensive and effective
         from all points of view. (644) Moreover, commitments in Phase I can only be accepted
         where the competition problem is readily identifiable and can easily be remedied. The
         competition problem therefore needs to be so straightforward, and the remedies so clear-
         cut, that it is not necessary to enter into an in-depth investigation and that the
         commitments are sufficient to clearly rule out ‘serious doubts’ within the meaning of
         Article 6(1)(c) of the Merger Regulation. (645)
(604)    In assessing whether the proposed commitments will maintain effective competition, the
         Commission considers all relevant factors, including the type, scale and scope of the
         proposed commitments with reference to the structure and the particular characteristics
         of the market in which the competition concerns arise, including the position of the
         parties and other participants on the market. (646) In order for the proposed commitments
         to comply with those principles, they must be capable of being implemented effectively
         within a short period of time. (647)
(605)    Concerning the type of acceptable commitments, the Merger Regulation gives discretion
         to the Commission as long as the commitments meet the required standards. Structural
         commitments will meet the conditions set out above only in so far as the Commission is
         able to conclude with the requisite degree of certainty, at the time of its decision, that it
         will be possible to implement them and that it will be likely that the new commercial
         structures resulting from them will be sufficiently workable and lasting to ensure that
         effective competition will be maintained. (648)

7.2.     Initial Commitments
(606)    In order to render the concentration compatible with the internal market, the Parties have
         modified the notified Transaction by entering into the commitments that the Notifying
         Party submitted on 30 June 2026 (the ‘Initial Commitments’) pursuant to Article 6(2)
         of the Merger Regulation.
(607)    The Initial Commitments provide for a mechanism through which Paramount will exit
         theatrical distribution of films through UIP in the EEA. In addition, for ten years,
         Paramount will not enter into a joint venture partnership or an equivalent arrangement
         engaged in the theatrical co-distribution of films in the EEA. In addition, the merged
         entity will not distribute through the same third-party distributors as other major US
         studios, i.e., Universal and/or Disney in the UIP Countries for the duration of 10 years.
(608)    Under the Initial Commitments Paramount will:
         (a)    As a first exit mechanism: reach an agreement with Universal (the ‘Amendment
                Agreement’) to amend the joint theatrical distribution arrangement between

(644)   Remedies Notice, paragraphs 9 and 61.
(645)   Remedies Notice, paragraph 81.
(646)   Remedies Notice, paragraph 12.
(647)   Remedies Notice, paragraph 9.
(648)   Remedies Notice, paragraph 10.
                                                    121
              Paramount and Universal under the UIP shareholders agreement (the ‘SHA’) and
              Paramount’s nominee agreement with UIP (the ‘Nominee Agreement’, together
              with the SHA, the ‘UIP governing agreements’) to facilitate Paramount’s prompt
              withdrawal from all EEA countries where Paramount currently distributes through
              UIP. The Amendment Agreement shall be concluded promptly after the
              Commission’s Decision pursuant to Article 6(1)(b) of the Merger Regulation (the
              ‘Effective Date’), and in any event, no later than a certain date within the third
              quarter of 2026. Under this termination mechanism, exit from the JV would be
              achieved within 13 months from closing of the Transaction (‘Closing’).
        (b)   As a “fall-back” exit mechanism: Paramount would unilaterally terminate the
              Nominee Agreement with UIP, if the Amendment Agreement is not executed by a
              certain date within the third quarter of 2026, by serving a termination notice within
              a fixed time period from Closing, and at the earliest date permitted under the UIP
              governing agreements. The termination of the Nominee Agreement will trigger the
              process provided for in the UIP SHA, leading to a complete exit by Paramount
              from the UIP joint venture. Under this termination mechanism, exit from the JV
              would be achieved at the earliest on [fourth quarter of 2027] if Closing occurs in
              2026 and on [fourth quarter of 2028] if Closing occurs in 2027.
(609)   For the avoidance of doubt, the termination of the Nominee Agreement shall be pursued
        only as a “fall-back” option, if the Amendment Agreement contemplated under (a) above
        has not been executed by a certain date within the third quarter of 2026.
(610)   The Initial Commitments also provide that Paramount, for a period of ten years from the
        Effective Date, will not, directly or indirectly:
        (a)   enter into any agreement, arrangement or understanding with Universal for the
              purpose of establishing a joint venture partnership engaged in the theatrical co-
              distribution of films in the EEA;
        (b)   in each EEA country where UIP operates through local offices or through a licensee
              arrangement with a third-party distributor, shift the distribution of Warner’s films
              for theatrical release from Warner’s existing distributor in any UIP Country to the
              theatrical distributor used by Paramount, where that distributor also distributes
              Universal’s or Disney’s films in that same UIP Country; and
        (c)   in the UIP Countries where Paramount and Universal do not share the same
              theatrical licensees, shift the distribution of Paramount’s films for theatrical release
              from Paramount’s existing distributor in any UIP Country to the theatrical
              distributor used by Warner, where that distributor also distributes Universal’s or
              Disney’s films in that same UIP Country.
(611)   The Initial Commitments provide for ring-fencing measures from the date of the Closing
        until the date on which Paramount’s exit from UIP takes full effect (‘Hold Separate
        Period’), and they govern the separation between, Paramount and Universal – through
        UIP. In particular, Paramount shall implement, and procure the implementation within
        UIP, of all necessary measures to ensure that it does not receive any business secrets,
        know-how, or any commercial information of a proprietary nature that is not in the public
        domain (‘Confidential Information’) relating to the theatrical distribution of Universal’s
        films. During the same period, Paramount shall not disclose to Universal Confidential
        Information relating to the theatrical distribution of films produced by Paramount’s or
        Warner’s studio labels. During this period, Paramount shall maintain and, where
        applicable, procure that UIP maintains appropriate contractual, operational and technical
        safeguards to prevent exchanges of Confidential Information with Universal.


                                                   122
7.3.      Assessment of the Initial Commitments

7.3.1.    The Notifying Party’s views
(612)     The Notifying Party considers that the Initial Commitments:
          (a)    remove any perceived competition concerns in the putative market for the
                 distribution of U.S. films for theatrical release, as Paramount will exit theatrical
                 distribution of films through UIP in the EEA. Accordingly, neither Paramount’s
                 film slate nor the merged entity’s film slate will be distributed through UIP in the
                 EEA or share distributors with Universal through UIP;
          (b)    are comprehensive and effective because they sever every contractual tie between
                 Paramount and Universal in theatrical distribution of US films in the EEA,
                 eliminating the structural link that the Commission identified as the basis for its
                 concerns. Further, the ten-year restrictions on establishing a joint venture
                 partnership engaged in the theatrical co-distribution of films in the EEA, equivalent
                 arrangements in the EEA, and specified theatrical distribution shifts in respect of
                 the EEA prevent circumvention through alternative commercial structures. No
                 residual theatrical co-distribution links relevant to the Commission’s perceived
                 concerns will remain between the merged entity and Universal, and there will be
                 no potential consolidation of the distribution of Paramount’s, Warner’s,
                 Universal’s and Disney’s slates under a single third-party distribution entity;
          (c)    can be implemented within a short period of time. If the Amendment Agreement is
                 concluded, the contractual restrictions in the SHA on the number and types of
                 territories that may be exited at the same time under the SHA will not apply,
                 enabling termination notices for all relevant EEA UIP Countries to be served in
                 parallel, with the applicable notice periods running concurrently. If the Amendment
                 Agreement is not executed by a certain date within the third quarter of 2026,
                 Paramount will unilaterally terminate the Nominee Agreement with UIP, which
                 automatically triggers a mechanism under the SHA that will result in Paramount’s
                 complete exit from UIP at the earliest possible date permitted under the UIP
                 governing agreements (i.e., [fourth quarter of 2027] if Closing occurs in 2026).
(613)     The Notifying Party considers that competition in the EEA will remain fully preserved
          during the Hold Separate Period. UIP is a cost-sharing vehicle subject to strict structural
          safeguards that will continue to be in place throughout the transition period. The Initial
          Commitments also provide for strict ring-fencing obligations, including safeguards to
          prevent exchanges of Confidential Information, preserve independent commercial
          decision-making, and prevent UIP from negotiating jointly on behalf of Paramount and
          Universal or leveraging a combined slate vis-à-vis cinemas.

7.3.2.    The results of the market test
(614)     The Commission launched a market test of the Initial Commitments on 1 July 2026.
(615)     The majority of the respondents to the Commission’s market test confirmed that:
          (a)    the provisions of the commitments are sufficiently clear and capable of being
                 implemented; (649)
          (b)    there are no difficulties or risks for the implementation of the commitments; (650)
          (c)    the commitments will be implemented in a sufficiently timely manner; (651)

(649)    Replies to Questionnaire ‘Q5- Remedies Market test’, question D.1.
(650)    Replies to Questionnaire ‘Q5- Remedies Market test’, question D.2.
(651)    Replies to Questionnaire ‘Q5- Remedies Market test’, question D.3.
                                                          123
          (d)    the ringfencing obligations during the Hold Separate Period, are sufficient; (652) and
          (e)    the duration of 10 years of the related commitments is sufficient. (653)
(616)     The feedback from the market investigation was mixed as regards to whether the Initial
          Commitments are sufficient to resolve the identified competition concerns. However, the
          Commission notes that the market participants’ concerns primarily relate to aspects
          beyond the Commission’s concerns identified in the market for the distribution of US
          films in the UIP Countries, such as output reduction, shortening of theatrical windows,
          loss of diversity, coordination between studios and distribution concerns in Non-UIP
          countries or distribution concerning resulting from the mere combination of Warner’s
          must-have portfolio with Paramount’s portfolio (regardless of whether they would be
          distributed with portfolios of other studios). (654)
(617)     Further, respondents to the Commission’s market test identified some shortcomings of
          the Initial Commitments, namely, that it would be necessary:
          (a)    in the UIP Countries where there is no shared licensee, i.e., where Paramount and
                 Universal currently do not both use the UIP licensee, to add a commitment making
                 clear that Warner’s films cannot be shifted to a distributor that already distributes
                 for Universal and/or Disney. Currently, the provision applies only to Paramount
                 shifting its film portfolio. However, a similar obligation should also apply to
                 Warner’s portfolio to ensure that the merged entity does not distribute with
                 Universal and/or Disney. This makes a difference, for example, in Portugal. In
                 particular, in Portugal, Paramount and Disney distribute through NOS while
                 Warner and Universal distribute through Cinemundo. The commitments currently
                 prevent Paramount from moving to Cinemundo but do not impose the same
                 limitation on Warner, i.e., Warner’s film slate could be moved to NOS, which
                 already distributes for Paramount and Disney; (655)
          (b)    to provide additional guarantees that the first exit mechanism, i.e., through the
                 Amendment Agreement with Universal is followed. Under the alternative exit
                 mechanism (unilateral termination mechanism), the implementation of the
                 commitments would take at least [15-20] months, i.e., would be achieved at the
                 earliest by [fourth quarter of 2027]; (656)
          (c)    to cover Sony in the commitments in addition to Universal and Disney, i.e., to
                 ensure that post-Transaction, the merged entity would not be able to distribute with
                 Universal, Disney and/or Sony; and (657)
          (d)    to clarify the type of Confidential Information that cannot be exchanged between
                 Paramount and Universal and, in particular, clarify that information on rental rates
                 and other key commercial terms cannot be exchanged. (658)

7.3.3.    The Commission’s assessment of the Initial Commitments
(618)     In light of the results of the market test of the Initial Commitments, the Commission
          concludes that the Initial Commitments are sufficiently clear and suitable to remove the
          grounds for serious doubts as to the compatibility of the Transaction with the internal

(658)    Replies to Questionnaire ‘Q5- Remedies Market test’, question D.7.1.
                                                          124
         market in the market for distribution of US films for theatrical release in UIP Countries,
         subject to the following reservations:
         (a)    As indicated by the respondents to the market test, the Initial Commitments
                contained a drafting gap in relation to the UIP Non-Shared Licensee Countries, as
                the relevant provision ensured that Paramount’s films cannot be shifted to a
                distributor that already distributes for Universal and/or Disney but did not impose
                an equivalent obligation on Warner’s portfolio.
         (b)    As indicated by the respondents, in case the first termination mechanism fails
                (because the Amendment Agreement with Universal cannot be concluded), the
                fallback unilateral termination mechanism produces complete exit only by [fourth
                quarter of 2027], i.e., within [15-20] months, lengthening the implementation
                timeline of the Initial Commitments and having a potential impact on the efficiency
                of the ring-fencing measures given their long duration. The Commission considers
                that the Notifying Party should introduce a clause enabling migration of
                Paramount’s portfolio within the same timeline as the implementation timeline
                under the first scenario, i.e., 13 months as of Closing. That would shorten the length
                of the ring-fencing measures and align the termination timelines under both
                termination mechanisms, ensuring timelier implementation of the Final
                Commitments.
         (c)    The Notifying Party should shorten the time period within which the notice
                terminating the Nominee Agreement and triggering the unilateral termination
                mechanism (‘Nominee Termination Notice’) should be served. The wording in the
                Initial Commitments specified that the Nominee Termination Notice needs to be
                provided within 30 calendar days from Closing (and as of 1 September). However,
                it also provides that the Nominee Termination Notice needs to be served between
                [within the applicable second-half calendar-year notice window] in any calendar
                year. That also implies that if closing of the Transaction occurs on the [date], under
                the text of the Initial Commitments, Paramount could only serve the Nominee
                Termination Notice in [third quarter of 2027]. The Commission considers that the
                [20-40]-day period in this scenario should be shortened in order to ensure that if
                closing falls at any point in [date], the Nominee Termination Notice is also served
                before [fourth quarter of 2026]. If closing falls at any point between [the first and
                the third quarter of 2027], the Nominee Termination Notice needs to be served on
                [third quarter of 2027].
         (d)    The Notifying Party should clarify the type of Confidential Information that cannot
                be exchanged between Paramount and Universal and clarify that information on
                rental rates and other key commercial terms cannot be exchanged. (659)
(619)    As regards the respondents’ suggestion for the Final Commitments to cover Sony, the
         Commission concludes that this is not required for the following reasons:
(620)    The Commission recalls that as discussed in Section 6.4.3.2, the Commission’s concern
         in the market for the distribution of US films to exhibitors in the UIP Countries is related
         to the current structural links between Paramount and Universal to which Warner’s
         portfolio would be added post-Transaction. By including Universal and Disney in Section
         C of the Initial Commitments, the Commission merely sought to avoid a situation where,
         as result of the termination of the UIP joint venture, the merged entity would share the
         same distributor as other large US film studios, i.e., Universal and Disney.
(621)    In that respect, as shown in Table 1 above, the Commission notes that Sony is a smaller
         player than Disney and Universal. Sony’s EEA-wide share of production and licensing

(659)   Replies to Questionnaire ‘Q5- Remedies Market test’, question D.7.1.
                                                         125
          of US films (based on box office revenue) was [5-10]% over the period of 2023-2025.
          When all studios are taken into account, Sony’s EEA-wide box office share was [5-10]%
          in 2024 and [0-5]% on 2025, with an average share of [5-10]% over the period of
          2023-2025.
(622)     Further, a review of the Sony distributors’ shares in the UIP Countries where Paramount
          would likely engage with the current Sony distributor to distribute the merged entity’s
          slate shows that the resulting combined share will remain moderate, i.e., below [30-40]%
          across all countries. (660)
(623)     On 9 July 2026, the Commission provided feedback to the Parties on the results of the
          market test and the Commission’s own assessment of the Initial Commitments.

7.4.      Assessment of the Final Commitments
(624)     On 16 July 2026, the Parties amended the Initial Commitments to take into account the
          comments received from the Commission based on the feedback from the market test
          and the Commission’s own assessment of the Initial Commitments (the ‘Final
          Commitments’). The Final Commitments are annexed to this decision and form an
          integral part thereof.
(625)     The Final Commitments do not contain any substantial changes to the Initial
          Commitments that would require a new market test. The Final Commitments contain the
          following improvements to the Initial Commitments:
          (a)    Addition of a new clause in paragraph 9 of the Final Commitments, fourth bullet
                 point, clarifying that in the UIP Countries where there is no shared licensee,
                 Warner’s films cannot be shifted to a distributor that already distributes for
                 Universal and/or Disney.
          (b)    Addition of a new paragraph 6 of the Final Commitments, clarifying that in case
                 the Amendment Agreement cannot be concluded by [third quarter of 2026] and if
                 the alternative unilateral termination scenario is followed, Paramount’s film
                 portfolio will be migrated away from the UIP OpCos and UIP Licensees within
                 13 months as of Closing, i.e., following the same shorter implementation timeline
                 as for the termination of the UIP OpCos under the first termination scenario, i.e.,
                 conclusion of the Amendment Agreement with Universal.
          (c)    Clarification of the time period when the Nominee Termination Notice needs to be
                 served: The Final Commitments clarify that if Closing falls at any point between
                 [period] in a given year, the Nominee Termination Notice shall be served on
                 31 August of that same year. If closing occurs between [period], the Nominee
                 Termination Notice shall be served within [20-40] calendar days from closing, but
                 no later than [fourth quarter] of that same year.
          (d)    Clarifying that in the Hold Separate Period, the following types of Confidential
                 Information cannot be exchanged between Paramount and Universal: non-public
                 information concerning release dates, release plans, release schedules, distribution
                 plans, budgets, marketing plans, rental terms and other booking terms, including a
                 number of contractual, operational and technical safeguards to prevent exchanges
                 of Confidential Information with Universal.

7.4.1.    Suitability of the Final Commitments to remove serious doubts
(626)     First, the Final Commitments remove competition concerns in the market for the
          distribution of US films to cinemas in the UIP Countries.

(660)    Response to RFI 27, question 1.
                                                     126
(627)   In the first place, Paramount will exit theatrical distribution of films through UIP in the
        EEA. Neither Paramount’s film slate nor the merged entity’s film slate will be distributed
        through UIP in the EEA or UIP Shared Licensees.
(628)   In the second place, the merged entity’s portfolio will not be distributed through
        distributors holding Universal and/or Disney’s portfolio in the UIP Countries, which in
        the alternative, could lead to a concentration of film portfolios in the hands of one
        distributor, leading to increased bargaining power vis-à-vis cinemas. Consequently, the
        Final Commitments are a sufficient measure to ensure that the merged entity’s (or the
        merged entity’s third-party distributor’s) bargaining power vis-à-vis cinemas does not
        increase post-Transaction in the UIP Countries in the EEA as a result of the addition of
        Warner’s film portfolio to Paramount’s and Universal’s film portfolios.
(629)   In the third place, if Disney or Universal change their third-party distributor in the next
        10 years, the merged entity must react accordingly and shift its distribution away from
        that distributor in order to ensure continuous compliance with the Final Commitments.
(630)   Second, the Final Commitments are comprehensive and effective because they sever
        every contractual tie and eliminate structural links between Paramount and Universal.
(631)   Third, the ten-year restrictions on establishing a joint venture partnership engaged in the
        theatrical co-distribution of films in the EEA, equivalent arrangements in the EEA
        prevent circumvention through alternative commercial structures.
(632)   Fourth, the implementation of the Final Commitments will be timely. Two considerations
        are relevant in this respect.
(633)   In the first place, the Parties have addressed the feedback point from the market test
        concerning the long implementation period under the alternative termination mechanism,
        i.e., unilateral termination. In the Final Commitments, the Parties have clarified that in
        case the Amendment Agreement cannot be concluded by [third quarter of 2026] and if
        the unilateral termination scenario is followed, Paramount’s film portfolio will be
        migrated away from the UIP OpCos and UIP Licensees within 13 months as of Closing,
        i.e., following the same shorter implementation timeline as for the termination of the UIP
        OpCos under the first termination scenario, i.e., conclusion of the Amendment
        Agreement with Universal. Accordingly, it is irrelevant which termination mechanism is
        followed as implementation of the Final Commitments will be achieved within
        13 months.
(634)   In the second place, the Parties clarified when the Nominee Termination Notice needs to
        be served in order to avoid any undue delay regarding the implementation of the Final
        Commitments. Therefore, even if Closing occurs in [date within the fourth quarter of
        2026], it has been clarified that the Nominee Termination Notice still needs to be served
        by [fourth quarter of 2026].
(635)   Fifth, the Final Commitments provide for strict ring-fencing obligations between
        Paramount and Universal in the interim period, including safeguards to prevent
        exchanges of confidential information, including information on rental rates, release
        dates, plans, schedules, distribution plans, budgets, marketing plans or booking terms.
(636)   Finally, the Monitoring Trustee will monitor Paramount’s and Warner’s compliance with
        the Final Commitments, including compliance with the ring-fencing measures in the
        interim period.
(637)   For the reasons outlined above, the Final Commitments are therefore sufficient to rule
        out serious doubts as to the compatibility of the Transaction with the internal market in
        the market for the distribution of US films to cinemas in the UIP Countries.

                                                  127
7.4.2.   Conclusion on the Final Commitments
(638)    The Commission considers that the improvements made in the Final Commitments
         adequately address the shortcomings identified with respect to the Initial Commitments.
(639)    Based on the results of the market test and the Commission’s assessment of the Initial
         Commitments and the Final Commitments, the Commission considers that the Final
         Commitments remove the Commission’s serious doubts as to the compatibility of the
         Transaction with the internal market.


8.       CONDITIONS AND OBLIGATIONS
(640)    Pursuant to the second subparagraph of Article 6(2) of the Merger Regulation, the
         Commission may attach to its decision conditions and obligations intended to ensure that
         the undertakings concerned comply with the commitments they have entered into vis-à-
         vis the Commission with a view to rendering the concentration compatible with the
         internal market.
(641)    The fulfilment of the measure that gives rise to the structural change of the market is a
         condition, whereas the implementing steps which are necessary to achieve this result are
         generally obligations on the Parties. Where a condition is not fulfilled, the Commission’s
         decision declaring the concentration compatible with the internal market is no longer
         applicable. Where the undertakings concerned commits a breach of an obligation, the
         Commission may revoke the clearance decision in accordance with Article 6(3)(b) of the
         Merger Regulation. The undertakings concerned may also be subject to fines and periodic
         penalty payments under Articles 14(2) and 15(1) of the Merger Regulation.
(642)    In accordance with the basic distinction described in the previous paragraph as regards
         conditions and obligations, this decision should be made conditional, on the one hand,
         on the full compliance by the Parties with Sections B and C (including Schedules 1.A,
         1.B and 2) of the Final Commitments submitted by the Parties on 12 July 2026. All other
         Sections of the Final Commitments should be obligations within the meaning of
         Article 6(2) of the Merger Regulation. The full text of the Final Commitments is attached
         as Annex A to this decision and forms an integral part thereof.


9.       CONCLUSION
(643)    For the above reasons, the Commission has decided not to oppose to the notified
         operation as modified by the commitments and to declare it compatible with the internal
         market and with the functioning of the EEA Agreement, subject to full compliance with
         the conditions in Sections B and C (including Schedules 1.A, 1.B and 2) of the
         commitments annexed to the present decision and with the obligations contained in the
         other sections of the said commitments. This decision is adopted in application of
         Article 6(1)(b) in conjunction with Article 6(2) of the Merger Regulation and Article 57
         of the EEA Agreement.

     Case M.12278 – PARAMOUNT SKYDANCE – WARNER BROS DISCOVERY


                  COMMITMENTS TO THE EUROPEAN COMMISSION


 Pursuant to Article 6(2) of Council Regulation (EC) No. 139/2004 (the “Merger Regulation”),
 Paramount Skydance Corporation (“PSKY”) hereby enters into the following Commitments (the
 “Commitments”) vis-à-vis the European Commission (the “Commission”) with a view to rendering the
 acquisition of sole control by PSKY of Warner Bros. Discovery, Inc. (“WBD”, and together with PSKY,
 the “Parties”) (the “Concentration”) compatible with the internal market and the functioning of the EEA
 Agreement.

 This text shall be interpreted in light of the Commission’s decision pursuant to Article 6(1)(b) of the
 Merger Regulation to declare the Concentration compatible with the internal market and the functioning
 of the EEA Agreement (the “Decision”), in the general framework of European Union law, in particular
 in light of the Merger Regulation, and by reference to the Commission Notice on remedies acceptable
 under Council Regulation (EC) No 139/2004 and under Commission Regulation (EC) No 802/2004 (the
 “Remedies Notice”).

 Section A. Definitions

1.     For the purposes of the Commitments, the following terms shall have the following meaning:

       Affiliated Undertakings: undertakings controlled by the Parties and/or by the ultimate
       parents of the Parties, whereby the notion of control shall be interpreted pursuant to
       Article 3 of the Merger Regulation and in light of the Commission Consolidated
       Jurisdictional Notice under Council Regulation (EC) No 139/2004 on the control of
       concentrations between undertakings (the "Consolidated Jurisdictional Notice").

       Amendment Agreement: the arrangement between the Committing Party (via its subsidiary
       PPIL) and NBCUniversal (via its subsidiary USL) to amend the joint theatrical distribution
       arrangement under the SHA and PPIL’s Nominee Agreement to facilitate PPIL’s prompt
       withdrawal from UIP OpCos and UIP Shared Licensees in EEA countries.

       Appointed Licensee: means, for each relevant UIP Country, the theatrical distributor identified
       for that UIP Country in Schedules 1.A, 1.B and 2, as applicable.

       Closing: the date the acquisition of sole control by PSKY of WBD takes legal effect.

       Committing Party: PSKY and, where the Commitments relate to a business on the Target side
       or include any obligations incumbent on the Target, the undertaking that is the Target of the
       Concentration. This also includes any legal successors of the Committing Party (notably the
       merged entity).

       Confidential Information: any business secrets, know-how, or any commercial information of
       a proprietary nature that is not in the public domain.

Section B.    The commitment to exit theatrical distribution through UIP in the EEA

3.    The Committing Party commits that PPIL will withdraw from UIP OpCos and UIP Licensees in
      EEA countries at the earliest date permitted under the SHA and Nominee Agreement, including
      any amendments thereto that are then in effect, by executing one of the options contemplated in
      paragraphs 4 and 5 below and, where applicable, by complying with paragraphs 6 and 7 below.
      For the avoidance of doubt, paragraphs 5 to 7 shall be pursued only as “fall-back” option if the

4.   Amendment of the joint theatrical distribution arrangement between UIP, PPIL, and USL

     Amendment Agreement is executed promptly after the Effective Date, and in any event, [in the

     Agreement, the restrictions currently set forth in Sections […] of the SHA – [number and types

            relevant Non-Shared Licensee Agreement, with termination to take effect at the earliest
            date permitted under the relevant agreement ([between three to six] months, as applicable
            to that agreement, following delivery of the relevant termination notice).

     •      [Confidential information regarding the applicable election options under the SHA].

     the third quarter of 2026], the Committing Party shall procure that PPIL unilaterally terminates
     the Nominee Agreement at the earliest date permitted thereunder, by serving a written notice of
     termination on UIP under Section […] of the Nominee Agreement (“Nominee Termination
     Notice”) within [20-40] calendar days from Closing, and no later than [the fourth quarter of any
     given year]. The Nominee Termination Notice must be provided [within the applicable second-
     half calendar-year notice window]. The Nominee Termination Notice shall trigger the
     Committing Party’s exit from UIP pursuant to Section […] of the SHA and automatically initiate

     [confidential information regarding the applicable SHA mechanism for implementing the
     transfer or wind-down of the Committing Party’s interest in UIP]. The main steps are as

     an extension limited to the affected UIP Country or UIP Countries. Any extension shall be limited
     to the shortest period necessary and shall not result in an Early Withdrawal Deadline later [three

8.   For the avoidance of doubt, the Committing Party reserves the right to (i) reinstate the UIP joint

                                                      5
SECTION C. RELATED COMMITMENTS

 9.    In furtherance of and to support the foregoing, the Committing Party further commits that it will
       not, within ten years from the Effective Date, directly or indirectly:

       •       Enter into any agreement, arrangement, or understanding with NBCUniversal (including
               any of its Affiliated Undertakings) for the purpose of establishing a joint venture
               partnership or similar commercial vehicle engaged in the theatrical co-distribution of films
               in the EEA;

       •       Shift the distribution of films for theatrical release produced by WBD’s Studio Labels from
               the Appointed Licensee used by WBD in each relevant UIP Country in Schedule 1.B to the
               Appointed Licensee in Schedule 1.A that also distributes NBCUniversal’s or Disney’s films
               in that UIP Country;

       •       Shift the distribution of films for theatrical release produced by PSKY’s Studio Labels from
               the Appointed Licensee used by PSKY in each relevant UIP Country in Schedule 2 to the
               Appointed Licensee used by WBD in that UIP Country, as listed in Schedule 2, where that
               licensee also distributes NBCUniversal’s or Disney’s films in that UIP Country.

       •       Shift the distribution of films for theatrical release produced by WBD’s Studio Labels from
               the Appointed Licensee used by WBD in each relevant UIP Country in Schedule 2 to the
               Appointed Licensee used by PSKY in that UIP Country, as listed in Schedule 2, where that
               licensee also distributes NBCUniversal’s or Disney’s films in that UIP Country

       Ring-fencing obligations

 10.   The Committing Party notes that within the SHA and the Nominee Agreement, the Committing
       Party (through PPIL) and NBCUniversal (through USL) operate fully independently within UIP,
       i.e., any commercially sensitive information is strictly ring-fenced, each studio retains sole control
       over the distribution of its own films, and UIP is contractually prohibited from negotiating jointly
       on behalf of both studios or leveraging a combined slate vis-à-vis exhibitors.


 11.   In any event, from the date of the Closing until the date on which the Committing Party’s exit from
       the joint theatrical distribution arrangement between PPIL and USL takes full effect, the ring-
       fencing obligations set out in this paragraph shall govern the separation between, on the one hand,
       the Committing Party – including PPIL and its Affiliated Undertakings – and, on the other hand,
       NBCUniversal – including USL and its Affiliated Undertakings – through UIP. The Committing
       Party shall implement, and procure the implementation within UIP, of all necessary measures to
       ensure that it does not receive Confidential Information relating to the theatrical distribution of
       NBCUniversal’s films, including non-public information concerning release dates, release plans,
       release schedules, distribution plans, budgets, marketing plans, rental terms and other booking
       terms. During the same period, the Committing Party shall not disclose to NBCUniversal
       Confidential Information relating to the theatrical distribution of films produced by PSKY’s Studio
       Labels or WBD’s Studio Labels. During this period, the Committing Party shall maintain and,
       where applicable, procure that UIP – including the relevant UIP OpCos and UIP Shared Licensees
       – maintains appropriate contractual, operational and technical safeguards to prevent exchanges of
       Confidential Information with NBCUniversal, including: [confidential information regarding
       contractual, operational and technical ring-fencing safeguards within UIP]. To that effect,


                                                        6
       within […] weeks of the Effective Date, the Committing Party shall provide a detailed work plan
       on the implementation of the ring-fencing measures to the Monitoring Trustee for its review.



SECTION D.     REPORTING

 12.   The Monitoring Trustee shall submit written reports in English confirming the Committing Party’s
       compliance with the Commitments to the Commission no later than two weeks (or at any other
       time requested by the Commission, if necessary) following Closing. Thereafter, until the
       withdrawal of PPIL from UIP OpCos and UIP Licensees in EEA countries as set out in paragraphs
       4 to 7 above, the Monitoring Trustee will submit compliance reports every six months (or at any
       other interim periods as requested by the Commission, if necessary) as well as a final compliance
       report within two weeks following such withdrawal. Following that final compliance report, the
       Monitoring Trustee shall submit an annual report to the Commission for the remainder of the term
       of the related commitments in Section C, limited to confirming whether those related commitments
       continue to be satisfied.



SECTION E.     MONITORING TRUSTEE

       I.        Appointment procedure


13.    The Committing Party shall appoint a Monitoring Trustee to carry out the functions specified in
       these Commitments for a Monitoring Trustee. The Committing Party commits not to close the
       Concentration before the appointment of a Monitoring Trustee.

14.    The Monitoring Trustee shall:

       (a)     at the time of appointment, be independent of the Parties and USL, and their
               Affiliated Undertakings;
       (b)     possess the necessary qualifications to carry out its mandate, for example have
               sufficient relevant experience as an investment banker or consultant or auditor;
       (c)     neither have nor become exposed to a Conflict of Interest;
       (d)     demonstrate sufficient capacity and resources to take on the Monitoring Trustee
               role with all the obligations that the role entails, including to report regularly to the
               Commission;
       (e)     have a sufficient presence in the EU/EEA, and sufficient expertise in the relevant
               EU/EEA countries covered by the Commitments.
15.    The Monitoring Trustee shall be remunerated by the Committing Party in a way that does not
       impede the independent and effective fulfilment of its mandate.

       Proposal by Committing Party
16.    No later than one week after the Effective Date, the Committing Party shall submit the names of
       at least three natural or legal persons whom the Committing Party proposes to appoint as the
       Monitoring Trustee to the Commission for approval. The proposal shall contain sufficient



                                                      7
      information for the Commission to verify that the persons proposed as Monitoring Trustee fulfil
      the requirements set out in paragraph 14 and shall include:

      (a)     the full terms of the proposed mandate, which shall include all provisions necessary
              to enable the Monitoring Trustee to fulfil its duties under these Commitments; and
      (b)     the outline of a work plan which describes how the Monitoring Trustee intends to
              carry out its assigned tasks.
      Approval or rejection by the Commission

17.   The Commission shall have the discretion to approve or reject the proposed Monitoring Trustee
      and to approve the proposed mandate subject to any modifications it deems necessary for the
      Monitoring Trustee to fulfil its obligations. If only one name is approved, the Committing Party
      shall appoint or cause to be appointed the person or persons concerned as Monitoring Trustee, in
      accordance with the mandate approved by the Commission. If more than one name is approved,
      the Committing Party shall be free to choose the Monitoring Trustee to be appointed from among
      the names approved. The Monitoring Trustee shall be appointed within one week of the
      Commission's approval, in accordance with the mandate approved by the Commission.

      New proposal by Committing Party

18.   If all the proposed Monitoring Trustees are rejected, the Committing Party shall submit the names
      of at least two more natural or legal persons within one week of being informed of the rejection,
      in accordance with paragraphs 13 and 17 of these Commitments.

      Monitoring Trustee nominated by the Commission

19.   If all further proposed Monitoring Trustees are rejected by the Commission, the Commission shall
      nominate a Monitoring Trustee, whom the Committing Party shall appoint, or cause to be
      appointed, in accordance with a trustee mandate approved by the Commission.

      II.       Functions of the Monitoring Trustee

20.   The Monitoring Trustee shall assume its specified duties and obligations in order to ensure
      compliance with the Commitments. The Monitoring Trustee will ensure that the Committing Party
      and UIP fully comply with the ring-fencing obligations set out in paragraphs 10 and 11, as well as
      in the SHA and the Nominee Agreement. The Commission may, on its own initiative or at the
      request of the Monitoring Trustee or the Committing Party, give any orders or instructions to the
      Monitoring Trustee in order to ensure compliance with the conditions and obligations attached to
      the Decision.

      III.      Duties and obligations of the Monitoring Trustee

21.   The Monitoring Trustee shall:

       (i)    propose in its first report to the Commission a detailed work plan describing how it intends
              to monitor compliance with the obligations and conditions attached to the Decision.

       (ii)   propose to the Committing Party such measures as the Monitoring Trustee considers
              necessary to ensure the Committing Party’s compliance with the conditions and
              obligations attached to the Decision;
                                                      8
      (iii)   promptly report in writing to the Commission, sending the Committing Party a non-
              confidential copy at the same time, if it concludes on reasonable grounds that the
              Committing Party is failing to comply with these Commitments. The Committing Party
              shall neither receive nor comment on draft reports that the Monitoring Trustee prepares
              for the purposes of reporting to the Commission;

      (iv)    promptly report in writing to the Commission as soon as it becomes aware of potential
              exposure to a Conflict of Interest appearing after its appointment, including if it becomes
              aware of any potential lack of independence or Conflict of Interest with any purchaser
              participating in the sale process;

      (v)     assume the other functions assigned to the Monitoring Trustee under the conditions and
              obligations attached to the Decision.

      IV.       Duties and obligations of the Committing Party


22.   The Committing Party shall provide, and shall cause its advisors to provide, the Monitoring Trustee
      with all such co-operation, assistance and information as the Monitoring Trustee may reasonably
      require to perform its tasks. The Monitoring Trustee shall have full and complete access to any of
      the Committing Party’s books, records, documents, management or other personnel, facilities, sites
      and technical information necessary for fulfilling its duties under the Commitments, and the
      Committing Party shall provide the Monitoring Trustee upon request with copies of any document.
      The Committing Party shall procure that UIP similarly provides full access to its books, records,
      documents, management or other personnel, facilities, sites and technical information necessary
      for fulfilling the Monitoring Trustee’s duties under the Commitments, and shall procure that UIP
      provides the Monitoring Trustee, upon request, with copies of any documents to which the
      Committing Party would itself have access. The Committing Party shall, and shall procure that
      UIP shall, make available to the Monitoring Trustee one or more offices on their premises and be
      available for meetings in order to provide the Monitoring Trustee with all information necessary
      for the performance of its tasks.

23.   The Committing Party shall provide the Monitoring Trustee with all managerial and administrative
      support that it may reasonably require while carrying out its duties. The Committing Party shall
      keep the Monitoring Trustee informed of all developments related to the implementation of
      Sections B and C of these Commitments. Following PPIL’s withdrawal from UIP OpCos and UIP
      Licensees in EEA countries as set out in paragraphs 4 to 7, the Monitoring Trustee’s continuing
      rights and the Committing Party’s corresponding obligations shall be limited to what is reasonably
      necessary to verify compliance with Section C. For that purpose, the Committing Party shall
      provide an annual certification to the Monitoring Trustee concerning the Appointed Licensees and
      other third-party distributors used by the Committing Party in the relevant UIP Countries to the
      extent necessary to confirm compliance with the Commitments in Section C. The Monitoring
      Trustee may rely on such certification for purposes of preparing its annual report to the
      Commission, but may request from the Committing Party any additional information, documents
      or explanations, and take any other steps, that it considers necessary to verify compliance with
      Section C.

24.   The Committing Party shall indemnify the Monitoring Trustee and its employees and agents (each
      an “Indemnified Party”) and hold each Indemnified Party harmless against, and hereby agrees
      that an Indemnified Party shall have no liability to the Committing Party for, any liabilities arising
      out of the performance of the Monitoring Trustee's duties under the Commitments, except to the

                                                       9
      extent that such liabilities result from the wilful default, recklessness, gross negligence or bad faith
      of the Monitoring Trustee, its employees, agents or advisors.

25.   At the expense of the Committing Party, the Monitoring Trustee may appoint advisors (in particular
      for corporate finance or legal advice), subject to the Committing Party’s approval (this approval
      not to be unreasonably withheld or delayed) if the Monitoring Trustee considers the appointment
      of such advisors necessary or appropriate for the performance of its duties and obligations under
      its mandate, provided that any fees and other expenses incurred by the Monitoring Trustee are in
      line with business practice. Should the Committing Party refuse to approve the advisors proposed
      by the Monitoring Trustee the Commission may approve the appointment of such advisors instead,
      at the expense of the Committing Party, after having heard the Committing Party. Only the
      Monitoring Trustee shall be entitled to issue instructions to the advisors. Paragraph 24 of these
      Commitments shall apply mutatis mutandis.

26.   The Committing Party agrees that the Commission may share Confidential Information proprietary
      to the Committing Party with the Monitoring Trustee. The Monitoring Trustee shall not disclose
      such information and the principles contained in Article 17(1) and (2) of the Merger Regulation
      apply mutatis mutandis.

27.   The Committing Party agrees that the contact details of the Monitoring Trustee are published on
      the website of the Commission’s Directorate-General for Competition, and it shall inform
      interested third parties of the identity and the tasks of the Monitoring Trustee.

28.   For a period of 10 years from the Effective Date the Commission may request all information from
      the Committing Party that is reasonably necessary to monitor the effective implementation of these
      Commitments.

      V.        Replacement, discharge and reappointment of the Monitoring Trustee


29.   If the Monitoring Trustee ceases to perform its functions under the Commitments or for any other
      good cause, including the exposure of the Monitoring Trustee to a Conflict of Interest:

      (a)     the Commission may, after hearing the Monitoring Trustee and the Committing
              Party, require the Committing Party to replace the Monitoring Trustee; or
      (b)     the Committing Party may, with the prior approval of the Commission, replace the
              Monitoring Trustee.
30.   If the Monitoring Trustee is removed according to paragraph 29 of these Commitments, the
      Monitoring Trustee may be required to continue in its function until a new Monitoring Trustee is
      in place to whom the Monitoring Trustee has effected a full hand over of all relevant information.
      The new Monitoring Trustee shall be appointed in accordance with the procedure referred to in
      paragraphs 13 - 19 of these Commitments.

31.   Unless removed according to paragraph 29 of these Commitments, the Monitoring Trustee shall
      cease to act as Monitoring Trustee only after the Commission has discharged it from its duties after
      all the Commitments with which the Monitoring Trustee has been entrusted have been
      implemented. However, the Commission may at any time require the reappointment of the
      Monitoring Trustee if it subsequently appears that the relevant remedies might not have been fully
      and properly implemented.



                                                        10
SECTION F.      INTERPRETATION OF THE COMMITMENTS


32.   In case of a conflict between the wording of the text of the present Commitments and the Amendment
      Agreement or any other contractual instrument executed by the Committing Party for the purposes
      of implementing the Commitments, the wording of the Commitments shall prevail, without
      prejudice to the fact that the interpretation of the Commitments shall take into account the solution
      that is more favourable to the effective implementation of the Commitments. Deviations from the
      Commitments in the Amendment Agreement or any such contractual instrument shall be valid only
      if expressly approved by the Commission.



SECTION G.      THE REVIEW CLAUSE


33.   The Commission may extend the time periods foreseen in paragraphs 4 to 7 of the Commitments in
      response to a request from the Committing Party or, in appropriate cases, on its own initiative. Where
      the Committing Party requests an extension of a time period, it shall submit a reasoned request to
      the Commission no later than a month before the expiry of that period, showing good cause. This
      request shall be accompanied by a report from the Monitoring Trustee, who shall, at the same time
      send a non-confidential copy of the report to the Committing Party. Only in exceptional
      circumstances shall the Committing Party be entitled to request an extension within the last month
      of any period.


34.   The Commission may further, in response to a reasoned request from the Committing Party showing
      good cause, waive, modify or substitute, in exceptional circumstances, one or more of the
      undertakings in these Commitments. This request shall be accompanied by a report from the
      Monitoring Trustee, who shall, at the same time send a non-confidential copy of the report to the
      Committing Party. The request shall not have the effect of suspending the application of the
      undertaking and, in particular, of suspending the expiry of any time period in which the undertaking
      has to be complied with.



SECTION H.      ENTRY INTO FORCE

35.   The Commitments shall take effect upon the date of adoption of the Decision.
